class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>. The reason given is: <b>All instances, except one, are before 2014. Of the ~ten cases here, eight occurred before 2010, making most of the 'enforcement' examples given well over 12 years old</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">December 2023</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table><span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt129"><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Wikipedia_articles_in_need_of_updating_from_December_2023"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_Wikipedia_articles_in_need_of_updating"/></span>
<p id="mw6g">In 2000, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Saudi_Arabia" title="Politics of Saudi Arabia" id="mw6w">Saudi Government</a> reported that it had sentenced nine Saudi men to extensive prison terms with lashing for engaging in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross-dressing" title="Cross-dressing" id="mw7A">cross-dressing</a> and homosexual relations.<sup about="#mwt133" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-33" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-33"}}'><a href="#cite_note-33" id="mw7Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw7g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7w">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8A">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw8Q">In 2001, Saudi teacher and playwright Muhammad Al-Suhaimi was charged with promoting homosexuality and after a trial was sentenced to prison. In 2006, he was given a pardon and allowed to resume teaching.<sup about="#mwt136" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-34" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-34"}}'><a href="#cite_note-34" id="mw8g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw8w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9A">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9Q">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw9g">In May 2005, the government arrested 92 men for homosexuality, who were given sentences ranging from fines to prison sentences of several months and lashings. Likewise, on 7 November 2005 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyadh" title="Riyadh" id="mw9w">Riyadh</a> police raided what the Saudi press called a "beauty contest for gay men" at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qatif" title="Al-Qatif" class="mw-redirect" id="mw-A">Al-Qatif</a>. What became of the five men arrested for organizing the event is not known.<sup about="#mwt139" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-35" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-35"}}'><a href="#cite_note-35" id="mw-Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw-g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-w">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_A">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw_Q">Persons caught living in the kingdom illegally are often accused of other crimes, involving illegal drugs, pornography, prostitution and homosexuality. Several such police crackdowns were reported in 2004–2005.<sup about="#mwt142" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-36" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-36"}}'><a href="#cite_note-36" id="mw_g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw_w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQA">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQE">]</span></span></a></sup> A similar raid in 2008 netted <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_people" title="Filipino people" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAQI">Filipino</a> workers arrested on charges of alcohol and gay prostitution.<sup about="#mwt145" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-arabnews1_37-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"arabnews1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-arabnews1-37"}}'><a href="#cite_note-arabnews1-37" id="mwAQM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQU">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQY">]</span></span></a></sup> The <i id="mwAQc"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_News" title="Arab News" id="mwAQg">Arab News</a></i> newspaper article on the arrests stated, "Gay rights are not recognized in the Middle East countries and the publication of any material promoting them is banned".<sup about="#mwt146" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-arabnews1_37-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"arabnews1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-arabnews1-37" id="mwAQk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQs">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAQ0">International protests from human rights organizations prompted some Saudi officials within the Saudi Arabian embassy in Washington, D.C., to imply that their kingdom will only use the death penalty when someone has been convicted of child molestation, rape, sexual assault, murder or engaging in anything deemed to be a form of political advocacy.<sup about="#mwt149" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-38" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-38"}}'><a href="#cite_note-38" id="mwAQ4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQ8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARA">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARE">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwARI">In 2010, Prince <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saud_bin_Abdulaziz_bin_Nasser_Al_Saud" title="Saud bin Abdulaziz bin Nasser Al Saud" class="mw-redirect" id="mwARM">Saud bin Abdulaziz bin Nasir al Saud</a> was found guilty at the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Bailey" title="Old Bailey" id="mwARQ">Old Bailey</a> in London of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder" title="Murder" id="mwARU">murdering</a> his servant Bandar Abdulaziz in their suite at the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Landmark_London" title="The Landmark London" id="mwARY">Landmark Hotel</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London" title="London" id="mwARc">London</a>.<sup about="#mwt152" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-BBC_News_39-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"BBC News"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-BBC_News-39"}}'><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News-39" id="mwARg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARo">[</span>38<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARs">]</span></span></a></sup> During the trial, it was alleged that the prince had received a "sexual massage" before the murder, and that he and Abdulaziz had been in a sexual relationship. According to the prosecutor, the Prince sexually and physically abused his servant as well as paid other men for sexual services.<sup about="#mwt155" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-40" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-40"}}'><a href="#cite_note-40" id="mwARw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAR0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAR4">[</span>39<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAR8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt158" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-41" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-41"}}'><a href="#cite_note-41" id="mwASA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASI">[</span>40<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASM">]</span></span></a></sup> He was sentenced to life imprisonment. In March 2013, he was allowed to return to Saudi Arabia to serve the remainder of his term in a Saudi prison. According to the agreement between the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom" id="mwASQ">United Kingdom</a> and Saudi Arabia, he must serve at least 20 years before he can be released.<sup about="#mwt161" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-42" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-42"}}'><a href="#cite_note-42" id="mwASU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASc">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwASk">In 2011–2012, the Saudi newspaper <i id="mwASo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Okaz" title="Okaz" id="mwASs">Okaz</a></i> announced that the government had arrested over 260 people for the crime of homosexuality over a one-year period. According to the official report, the men were caught cross-dressing, wearing ladies' makeup and trying to pick up other men.<sup about="#mwt164" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Stewart_2012_43-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Stewart 2012"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Stewart_2012-43"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Stewart_2012-43" id="mwASw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAS0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS4">[</span>42<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS8">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwATA">A gay Saudi diplomat named Ali Ahmad Asseri applied for asylum in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States" title="United States" id="mwATE">United States</a> after he claimed the Saudi government discovered his sexuality and also because of dissent.<sup about="#mwt167" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-44" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-44"}}'><a href="#cite_note-44" id="mwATI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATQ">[</span>43<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwATY">In 2014, a 24-year-old Saudi Arabian man was sentenced to three years' detention and 450 lashes after a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medina" title="Medina" id="mwATc">Medina</a> court found him guilty of "promoting the vice and practice of homosexuality", after he was caught using <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter" title="Twitter" class="mw-redirect" id="mwATg">Twitter</a> to arrange dates with other men.<sup about="#mwt170" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-twitter_45-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"twitter"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-twitter-45"}}'><a href="#cite_note-twitter-45" id="mwATk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATs">[</span>44<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAT0">In February 2017, 35 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistanis" title="Pakistanis" id="mwAT4">Pakistani</a> transgender women were arrested at a party in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyadh" title="Riyadh" id="mwAT8">Riyadh</a>.<sup about="#mwt173" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-46" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-46"}}'><a href="#cite_note-46" id="mwAUA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUI">[</span>45<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUM">]</span></span></a></sup> Two were allegedly forced into sacks and beaten to death with sticks by Saudi authorities.<sup about="#mwt176" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_47-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:0-47"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-47" id="mwAUQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUY">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUc">]</span></span></a></sup> State media reported that 35 men who were dressed as women and wearing make-up were arrested, but did not report that anyone from the event was executed, nor that any of the attendees were transgender.<sup about="#mwt177" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_47-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-47" id="mwAUg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUo">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUs">]</span></span></a></sup> Reports of the deaths had circulated in Pakistani media by March, but the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_(Saudi_Arabia)" title="Ministry of Interior (Saudi Arabia)" id="mwAUw">Saudi Interior Ministry</a> denied that anyone was executed and claimed that the reports were false.<sup about="#mwt180" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:1_48-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:1-48"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:1-48" id="mwAU0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAU4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAU8">[</span>47<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVA">]</span></span></a></sup> The ministry later said that a 61-year-old Pakistani from the event died while in custody after suffering from a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_attack" title="Heart attack" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVE">heart attack</a>.<sup about="#mwt181" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:1_48-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:1-48" id="mwAVI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVQ">[</span>47<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAVY">In April 2019, 37 Saudi men were <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decapitation" title="Decapitation" id="mwAVc">beheaded</a> on after confessing to terrorism and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espionage" title="Espionage" id="mwAVg">spying</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Saudi_Arabia_proxy_conflict" title="Iran–Saudi Arabia proxy conflict" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVk">on the country for Iran</a>.<sup about="#mwt184" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-49" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-49"}}'><a href="#cite_note-49" id="mwAVo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVw">[</span>48<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAV0">]</span></span></a></sup> One of those executed, admitted to having sexual relationships with four of the other accused men and organizing during a trial held in 2016. He also admitted to organizing violent anti-government protests. His motive was that he <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunniphobic" title="Sunniphobic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAV4">opposed to the Sunni Sect of Islam</a> due to his hatred of the state and the Saudi people.<sup about="#mwt187" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-50" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-50"}}'><a href="#cite_note-50" id="mwAV8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWE">[</span>49<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWI">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAWM">On 13 October 2019, Saudi authorities arrested a 23-year-old national, Suhail al-Jameel, for posting a picture of himself wearing short shorts at the beach. He is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay" title="Gay" id="mwAWQ">gay</a> social media personality in Saudi Arabia, who openly documented his sexuality on <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snapchat" title="Snapchat" id="mwAWU">Snapchat</a> and Twitter. Al-Jameel wrote on Snapchat that he was arrested on the charges of sharing nudity online. Soon after fans started using a #freesuhail hashtag on Twitter.<sup about="#mwt190" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-51" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-51"}}'><a href="#cite_note-51" id="mwAWY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWg">[</span>50<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWk">]</span></span></a></sup> He was released from prison in October 2022.<sup about="#mwt193" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-52" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-52"}}'><a href="#cite_note-52" id="mwAWo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWw">[</span>51<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAW0">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAW4">In July 2022, Tala Safwan, a female Egyptian <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Influencer" title="Influencer" id="mwAW8">influencer</a> living in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyadh" title="Riyadh" id="mwAXA">Riyadh</a>, posted a video chatting with a female Saudi friend. Safwan invited her friend to her house, saying that “everyone will be asleep” and that “no one will hear her scream.” Many in the country interpreted this as sexual innuendo, and Safwan was subsequently arrested.<sup about="#mwt196" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-53" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-53"}}'><a href="#cite_note-53" id="mwAXE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXM">[</span>52<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt199" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-54" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-54"}}'><a href="#cite_note-54" id="mwAXU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXc">[</span>53<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwAXk" aria-labelledby="Same-sex_relationships"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Same-sex_relationships">Same-sex relationships</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Same-sex relationships"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwAXo">Saudi Arabia bans <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage" title="Same-sex marriage" id="mwAXs">same-sex marriage</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_partnership" title="Domestic partnership" id="mwAXw">domestic partnerships</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_union" title="Civil union" id="mwAX0">civil unions</a>. The country neither recognizes nor performs these types of same-sex actions. The maximum punishment for same-sex behavior in Saudi Arabia is <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_for_homosexuality" title="Capital punishment for homosexuality" id="mwAX4">death</a>.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwAX8" aria-labelledby="Censorship"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Censorship">Censorship</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Censorship"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt200" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAYA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Censorship in Saudi Arabia"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt201" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt200" id="mwAYE">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship_in_Saudi_Arabia" title="Censorship in Saudi Arabia">Censorship in Saudi Arabia</a></div>

<p id="mwAYI">The Saudi government censors the media. Fines, imprisonment and, for foreigners, deportation are imposed on any person possessing, importing, distributing or producing media without governmental approval. Media content, including advertising, cannot be seen as insulting <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Saud" title="House of Saud" id="mwAYM">the royal family</a> or conflicting with Islamic teachings and values.<sup about="#mwt204" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-55" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-55"}}'><a href="#cite_note-55" id="mwAYQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYY">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYc">]</span></span></a></sup> Anything perceived to condone homosexuality or as support for LGBTQ rights can result in prosecution.<sup about="#mwt205" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-hrw.org_56-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"hrw.org"}}'><a href="#cite_note-hrw.org-56" id="mwAYg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYo">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYs">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAYw">Homosexuality and cross-dressing are dealt with in print news through news coverage of criminal matters, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS" id="mwAY0">HIV/AIDS</a> pandemic or allusions to perceived Western <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decadence" title="Decadence" id="mwAY4">decadence</a>.<sup about="#mwt208" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-57" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-57"}}'><a href="#cite_note-57" id="mwAY8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZE">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZI">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAZM">Radio and TV programs are similarly banned from expressing support for LGBTQ rights, but homosexuality and cross-dressing can be discussed as long as the negative attitudes and biases are reinforced.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt209" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAZQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"December 2021"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_December_2021" about="#mwt209"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt209" id="mwAZU"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup> For example, a call-in TV show may feature a discussion about the immorality or "illness" of homosexuality, or, as in the 2011 case of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirel_Radoi" title="Mirel Radoi" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAZY">Mirel Radoi</a>, a Romanian-born footballer playing in Saudi, coverage may focus on a celebrity expressing disapproving views. In the Radoi case, the footballer implied, as an insult, that an opponent he was complaining about was gay. (Radoi was fined and suspended from play for two matches by the Saudi football disciplinary committee for these remarks.)<sup about="#mwt212" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-58" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-58"}}'><a href="#cite_note-58" id="mwAZc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZk">[</span>57<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZo">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAZs">The government has allowed public movie theaters since 2018, but the films are censored, as are the DVDs purchased in many retail stores. LGBTQ themes are generally one of the themes edited out of movies. Customs agents keep a list of films or TV shows that are not allowed to be brought into the kingdom.<sup about="#mwt215" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-59" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-59"}}'><a href="#cite_note-59" id="mwAZw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZ0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZ4">[</span>58<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZ8">]</span></span></a></sup> Foreign films that include small acknowledgments of a character's LGBTQ identity are often banned in the kingdom, even if the content or depiction is not sexually explicit.<sup about="#mwt218" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-60" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-60"}}'><a href="#cite_note-60" id="mwAaA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAaE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaI">[</span>59<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAaQ">Government regulation of the Internet generally falls under the Royal Decrees on Anti-Cyber Crime (2007). Article 6 prohibits creating, distributing or accessing online content or webpages that the government deems to be <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pornographic" title="Pornographic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAaU">pornographic</a> or in violation of religious values or public morals or is a threat to public health, safety or order.<sup about="#mwt221" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-WIPO_2007_61-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"WIPO 2007"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-WIPO_2007-61"}}'><a href="#cite_note-WIPO_2007-61" id="mwAaY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAac"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAag">[</span>60<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAak">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAao">The Saudi government has frequently blocked Internet users in the kingdom from accessing web pages or other online content that express support for LGBTQ rights.<sup about="#mwt224" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-62" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-62"}}'><a href="#cite_note-62" id="mwAas"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAaw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa0">[</span>61<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa4">]</span></span></a></sup> The restrictions on the Internet extend to blogs, social media and video upload webpages.<sup about="#mwt227" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-63" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-63"}}'><a href="#cite_note-63" id="mwAa8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbE">[</span>62<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbI">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAbM">In 2010, a 27-year-old Saudi man was charged with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homosexuality" title="Homosexuality" id="mwAbQ">homosexuality</a> and impersonating a police officer when he posted a comical video of himself online, where he discusses popular culture, shows off his chest hair and flirts with the camera man. He was sentenced to a year in prison, with 1,000 lashes, and ordered to pay a fine of 5,000 rials (US$1,333).<sup about="#mwt230" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-64" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-64"}}'><a href="#cite_note-64" id="mwAbU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbc">[</span>63<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAbk">In a crackdown across stores in the Saudi capital, Riyadh, according to the Saudi state-run TV channel <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ekhbariya" title="Al-Ekhbariya" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAbo">al-Ekhbariya</a>, government officials seized rainbow-colored clothing, products, toys, etc. The officials claimed that the June 2022 move was aimed at curbing the direct and indirect promotion of homosexuality in the country. An unnamed official from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Commerce_(Saudi_Arabia)" title="Ministry of Commerce (Saudi Arabia)" id="mwAbs">Ministry of Commerce</a> claimed that they were looking out for "slogans that violate the rules of Islam and public morals like promoting homosexuality colors, targeting the young generation." The items seized in the crackdown included hair accessories for children, backpacks, pencil cases, and rainbow stripes featured on crayon packs. When asked for comments from the Saudi authorities, none were received.<sup about="#mwt233" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-65" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-65"}}'><a href="#cite_note-65" id="mwAbw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAb0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb4">[</span>64<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb8">]</span></span></a></sup> In December 2022, authorities in neighboring <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar" title="Qatar" id="mwAcA">Qatar</a> carried out a similar purge.<sup about="#mwt236" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-66" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-66"}}'><a href="#cite_note-66" id="mwAcE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcM">[</span>65<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwAcU" aria-labelledby="Gender_identity"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Gender_identity">Gender identity</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Gender identity"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwAcY">According to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch" id="mwAcc">Human Rights Watch</a>, Saudi Arabia has no laws against transgender people. Arrests and prosecutions of individuals wearing clothing not considered appropriate to their sex (cross-dressing), men wearing makeup, or "feminine" accessories, occur. Penalties include prison sentences and floggings.<sup about="#mwt240" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-67" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-67"}}'><a href="#cite_note-67" id="mwAcg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAck"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAco">[</span>66<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcs">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt243" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Reuters_68-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Reuters"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Reuters-68"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Reuters-68" id="mwAcw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAc0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAc4">[</span>67<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAc8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt244" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-IDuA_5-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"IDuA"}}'><a href="#cite_note-IDuA-5" id="mwAdA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdI">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap" about="#mwt237" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAdQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"rp","href":"./Template:Rp"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"207"}},"i":0}}]}'><span title="Page / location: 207"><span typeof="mw:Entity">:</span><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>207<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span></span></sup></p>

<p id="mwAdU">Over the past 36 years, more than 2,000 individuals in Saudi Arabia have undergone <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender-affirming_surgery" title="Gender-affirming surgery" id="mwAdY">sex reassignment surgeries</a> (also known as "sex-correction surgeries" in Saudi Arabia) due to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_dysphoria" title="Gender dysphoria" id="mwAdc">gender dysphoria</a>. However, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_(Saudi_Arabia)" title="Ministry of Health (Saudi Arabia)" id="mwAdg">Ministry of Health</a> does not permit sex change operations, by which a person with a clear gender identity and matching physical features seeks to change sex.<sup about="#mwt247" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-69" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-69"}}'><a href="#cite_note-69" id="mwAdk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAds">[</span>68<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAd0">In 2017, two <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender" title="Transgender" id="mwAd4">transgender</a> Pakistanis were allegedly "packed in sacks, thrashed with sticks and tortured to death" by Saudi police.<sup about="#mwt250" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ind_2017_70-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ind 2017"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-ind_2017-70"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ind_2017-70" id="mwAd8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAeA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeE">[</span>69<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeI">]</span></span></a></sup> A statement from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_(Saudi_Arabia)" title="Ministry of Interior (Saudi Arabia)" id="mwAeM">Saudi Interior Ministry</a>, however, said the reports were "totally wrong and nobody was tortured".<sup about="#mwt253" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-71" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-71"}}'><a href="#cite_note-71" id="mwAeQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAeU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeY">[</span>70<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAec">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt256" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-72" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-72"}}'><a href="#cite_note-72" id="mwAeg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAek"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeo">[</span>71<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAes">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAew">People with a transsexual or transgender identity cards are not allowed to make the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hajj" title="Hajj" id="mwAe0">pilgrimage to Mecca</a>.<sup about="#mwt259" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-73" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-73"}}'><a href="#cite_note-73" id="mwAe4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAe8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfA">[</span>72<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfE">]</span></span></a></sup> However, in February 2018 it was reported that a group of transgender individuals from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan" title="Pakistan" id="mwAfI">Pakistan</a> were allowed to be sent to Saudi Arabia for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hajj" title="Hajj" id="mwAfM">Hajj</a>.<sup about="#mwt262" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-74" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-74"}}'><a href="#cite_note-74" id="mwAfQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfY">[</span>73<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfc">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt263" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Reuters_68-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Reuters"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Reuters-68" id="mwAfg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfo">[</span>67<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfs">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAfw">In March 2023, a 23-year old Saudi transgender woman, named <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_of_Eden_Knight" title="Suicide of Eden Knight" id="mwAf0">Eden Knight</a>, died by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide" title="Suicide" id="mwAf4">suicide</a> after being <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detransition#Forced_detransition" title="Detransition" id="mwAf8">forced to detransition</a> by her family in Saudi Arabia.<sup about="#mwt266" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-75" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-75"}}'><a href="#cite_note-75" id="mwAgA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgI">[</span>74<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt269" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-76" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-76"}}'><a href="#cite_note-76" id="mwAgQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgY">[</span>75<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwAgg" aria-labelledby="Intersex_rights"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Intersex_rights">Intersex rights</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Intersex rights"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAgk">There are no <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodily_integrity" title="Bodily integrity" id="mwAgo">bodily protections</a> for intersex individuals in Saudi Arabia. Many intersex infants born with ambiguous genitalia suffer from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infanticide" title="Infanticide" id="mwAgs">infanticide</a>. The killing of intersex infants is primarily motivated by fears of the social <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shame" title="Shame" id="mwAgw">shame</a> of having an intersex child.<sup about="#mwt272" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-77" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-77"}}'><a href="#cite_note-77" id="mwAg0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAg4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAg8">[</span>76<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt275" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-78" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-78"}}'><a href="#cite_note-78" id="mwAhE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhM">[</span>77<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt278" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-79" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-79"}}'><a href="#cite_note-79" id="mwAhU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhc">[</span>78<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhg">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersex_genital_surgery" title="Intersex genital surgery" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAhk">Intersex genital surgery</a> on newborns for cosmetic purposes is fully legal and encouraged.<sup about="#mwt281" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-80" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-80"}}'><a href="#cite_note-80" id="mwAho"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhw">[</span>79<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAh0">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwAh4" aria-labelledby="Living_conditions"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Living_conditions">Living conditions</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Living conditions"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwAh8" aria-labelledby="Cultural_norms"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Cultural_norms">Cultural norms</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Cultural norms"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAiA">Unmarried women and families are generally kept separate from single men as much as possible, and dating is generally seen as being <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taboo" title="Taboo" id="mwAiE">taboo</a> and immoral. Opposite sex couples may be harassed if they demonstrate affection in public; however it is not uncommon to see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heterosexual" title="Heterosexual" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAiI">heterosexual</a> men seemingly expressing affection toward each other in public (e.g., kissing on the cheeks or holding hands.) The practice of men holding hands or kissing on the cheeks in public, is a social custom in parts of the Middle East and Asia; it is a symbol of friendship and not homosexuality.<sup about="#mwt284" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-81" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-81"}}'><a href="#cite_note-81" id="mwAiM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAiQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiU">[</span>80<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiY">]</span></span></a></sup> 
Bars and nightclubs are illegal. While the CPVPV views combating homosexuality as one of its major objectives, the targets of its interventions against improper socialising are more likely to be the mixed-sex, than the single-sex gathering.<sup about="#mwt285" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2010_sg_26-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"2010 sg"}}'><a href="#cite_note-2010_sg-26" id="mwAic"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAig"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAik">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAio">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwAis" aria-labelledby="Discrimination_and_harassment"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Discrimination_and_harassment">Discrimination and harassment</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Discrimination and harassment"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAiw">Saudi Arabia has laws against discrimination on the basis of gender<sup about="#mwt290" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-82" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-82"}}'><a href="#cite_note-82" id="mwAi0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAi4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAi8">[</span>81<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjA">]</span></span></a></sup> but not <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_orientation" title="Sexual orientation" id="mwAjE">sexual orientation</a>. Harassment or violence against LGBTQ people is not addressed in any bias-motivated or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_crime" title="Hate crime" id="mwAjI">hate crime</a> law. Advocacy for LGBTQ rights is illegal within the kingdom.
The required exit and entry visa paperwork does not ask people about their sexual orientation, as it does their <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationality" title="Nationality" id="mwAjM">nationality</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biological_sex" title="Biological sex" id="mwAjQ">sex</a>, religion and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marital_status" title="Marital status" id="mwAjU">marital status</a>. In 2011, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirel_Radoi" title="Mirel Radoi" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAjY">Mirel Radoi</a>, a Romanian football player who plays for the Saudi Alhilal Club, was fined 20,000 Saudi Riyals and suspended for two matches after calling a Saudi Arabian football player, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_Abdul_Ghani" title="Hussein Abdul Ghani" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAjc">Hussein Abdul Ghani</a>, who plays for Nasr Club, gay. The public comment, intended as an insult, was highly controversial and generated quite a bit of coverage in the Saudi press, including the refusal of Hussein Abdul Ghani to shake hands with Mirel Radoi after a later game.<sup about="#mwt293" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-83" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-83"}}'><a href="#cite_note-83" id="mwAjg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjo">[</span>82<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjs">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAjw">The climate of discrimination and fear among the LGBTQ community in Saudi Arabia means that only Saudis who have sought asylum abroad, such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdulrahman_Alkhiary" title="Abdulrahman Alkhiary" id="mwAj0">Abdulrahman Alkhiary</a> (also known as Wajeeh Lion), are able to publicly identify as gay. Alkhairy states that he is the first openly gay Saudi man.<sup about="#mwt296" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-84" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-84"}}'><a href="#cite_note-84" id="mwAj4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAj8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkA">[</span>83<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkE">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mwAkI" aria-labelledby="Education"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Education">Education</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Education"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAkM">Public education in Saudi Arabia is required to teach basic Islamic values based on the interpretation of the Quran, which includes a strong condemnation of homosexuality. In addition, Islam condemns cross-dressing. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_(Saudi_Arabia)" title="Ministry of Education (Saudi Arabia)" id="mwAkQ">Ministry of Education</a> approved textbooks that reflect the country's Islamic and cultural view against homosexual acts by stating that "homosexuality is one of the most disgusting sins and greatest crimes", and that the proper punishment for the intentional act of homosexual intercourse in public is capital punishment.<sup about="#mwt297" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-85" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-85"}}'><a href="#cite_note-85" id="mwAkU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAkY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkc">[</span>84<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkg">]</span></span></a></sup> However, in 2020 the Ministry of Education was found to have removed the condemnation of homosexuality as something punishable by death from textbooks.<sup about="#mwt300" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-86" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-86"}}'><a href="#cite_note-86" id="mwAkk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAko"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAks">[</span>85<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAk0">In 2012, the Saudi government asked the CPVPV to assist in the expulsion of students who were judged, by their mannerisms and taste in fashion, to be gay or lesbian.<sup about="#mwt303" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-HuffPost_2012_87-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"HuffPost 2012"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-HuffPost_2012-87"}}'><a href="#cite_note-HuffPost_2012-87" id="mwAk4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAk8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlA">[</span>86<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlE">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAlI">Private schools exist in Saudi Arabia, mainly to serve the needs of expatriates with children, and they are generally allowed more discretion in setting up their own school curriculum and policies. Unless a majority of the expatriate families are Muslim, the private school is likely to only teach the basic beliefs of Islam, through lessons about the culture, language and history of Saudi Arabia. Textbook content or policies regarding homosexuality or cross-dressing tends to be influenced by the prevailing attitudes of the expatriates and their country of origin.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt304" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAlM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"December 2023"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_December_2023" about="#mwt304"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt304" id="mwAlQ"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mwAlU" aria-labelledby="Clubs_and_associations"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Clubs_and_associations">Clubs and associations</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Clubs and associations"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwAlY">Appearing to support or endorse homosexuality or LGBTQ rights can result in prosecution.<sup about="#mwt307" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-hrw.org_56-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"hrw.org"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-hrw.org-56"}}'><a href="#cite_note-hrw.org-56" id="mwAlc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlk">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlo">]</span></span></a></sup> This applies to individuals as well as clubs or associations operating the kingdom.</p>

<p id="mwAls">In MDLBeast Soundstorm, an annual multi-day music festival in Riyadh, observers have reported that it included the public display of LGBTQI+ culture.<sup about="#mwt310" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-88" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-88"}}'><a href="#cite_note-88" id="mwAlw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAl0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAl4">[</span>87<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAl8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt313" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-89" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-89"}}'><a href="#cite_note-89" id="mwAmA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAmE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmI">[</span>88<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAmQ">The clandestine Green Party of Saudi Arabia, formed in 2001, expressed support for LGBTQ rights as part of its larger human rights platform.  It is unclear if the party still exists, as reports are that its members were subjected to a government crackdown. <link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt314" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAmU" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"October 2025"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_October_2025" about="#mwt314"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt314" id="mwAmY"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2025)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup>  A Facebook group for the party seems to still exist, as of 2024.</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="13" id="mwAmc" aria-labelledby="HIV/AIDS"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="HIV/AIDS"><span id="HIV.2FAIDS" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>HIV/AIDS</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: HIV/AIDS"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwAmg">Officially, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men_who_have_sex_with_men" title="Men who have sex with men" id="mwAmk">men who have sex with men</a> account for a low number of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV" title="HIV" id="mwAmo">HIV</a> cases in the Kingdom of Saudi Arabia and little in the way of preventative educational materials exist for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men_who_have_sex_with_men" title="Men who have sex with men" id="mwAms">men who have sex with men</a>, although a 2024 report from the Saudi AIDS National Program does make some mention of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men_who_have_sex_with_men" title="Men who have sex with men" id="mwAmw">men who have sex with men</a>.<sup about="#mwt317" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-90" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-90"}}'><a href="#cite_note-90" id="mwAm0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAm4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAm8">[</span>89<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAnE">Legally, Saudi citizens who are living with HIV/AIDS are classified as being disabled and thus are entitled to free medical care, which may include access <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-exposure_prophylaxis_for_HIV_prevention" title="Pre-exposure prophylaxis for HIV prevention" id="mwAnI">PrEP</a>, protection of their privacy (as to how they became infected), housing, and employment.</p>

<p id="mwAnM">The government has produced or allowed <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NGOs" title="NGOs" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAnQ">NGOs</a> to produce, educational material on how the disease has spread since the 1980s. Abdullah al-Hokail, a Saudi doctor who specializes in the pandemic, has been allowed to air public service announcements on television about the disease and how it is spread.<sup about="#mwt320" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-arabnews2006_91-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"arabnews2006"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-arabnews2006-91"}}'><a href="#cite_note-arabnews2006-91" id="mwAnU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnc">[</span>90<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAng">]</span></span></a></sup> Yet, ignorance, fear and prejudice are often directed at people living with the disease. While the government has designated several hospitals to treat those people infected with AIDS or HIV, other hospitals often refuse to care for such people or fail to treat them in a compassionate and humane manner.<sup about="#mwt323" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-92" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-92"}}'><a href="#cite_note-92" id="mwAnk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAno"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAns">[</span>91<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnw">]</span></span></a></sup> Hospitals and schools are often reluctant to distribute government information about the disease because of strong taboos and the stigma attached to how the virus can be spread.<sup about="#mwt326" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-93" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-93"}}'><a href="#cite_note-93" id="mwAn0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAn4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn8">[</span>92<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoA">]</span></span></a></sup> For example, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condom" title="Condom" id="mwAoE">condoms</a> are legal, but until recently, they were rarely available anywhere other than certain hospitals or medical supply stores.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="14" id="mwAoI" aria-labelledby="1990s"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="1990s">1990s</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: 1990s"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAoM">In the late 1990s, the Saudi government began to step up a public education campaign about AIDS-HIV slowly. It started to recognize <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_AIDS_Day" title="World AIDS Day" id="mwAoQ">World AIDS Day</a>, and the Arabic and English daily newspapers were permitted to run articles and opinions expressing the need for more education about the disease and more compassion for those infected.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="15" id="mwAoU" aria-labelledby="2003"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="2003">2003</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: 2003"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAoY">In 2003, the government announced that it knew of 6,787 cases, and in 2004 the official number rose to 7,808. The government statistics claim that most of the registered cases are foreign males who contracted the disease through "forbidden" sexual relations.<sup about="#mwt329" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-94" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-94"}}'><a href="#cite_note-94" id="mwAoc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAog"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAok">[</span>93<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoo">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="16" id="mwAos" aria-labelledby="2006"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="2006">2006</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: 2006"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAow">In June 2006, the Ministry of Health publicly admitted that more than 10,000 Saudi citizens were either infected with HIV or had AIDS.<sup about="#mwt332" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-nytimes2006_95-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"nytimes2006"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-nytimes2006-95"}}'><a href="#cite_note-nytimes2006-95" id="mwAo0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAo4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAo8">[</span>94<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApA">]</span></span></a></sup> In December 2006, the <i id="mwApE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_News" title="Arab News" id="mwApI">Arab News</a></i> ran an editorial that called for greater public awareness of how the virus is spread and more compassion for those people infected.<sup about="#mwt335" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-96" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-96"}}'><a href="#cite_note-96" id="mwApM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApU">[</span>95<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApY">]</span></span></a></sup> In the same year, a Saudi citizen named Rami al-Harithi revealed that he had become infected with HIV while having surgery and has become an official proponent of education and showing compassion to those people infected.<sup about="#mwt336" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-nytimes2006_95-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"nytimes2006"}}'><a href="#cite_note-nytimes2006-95" id="mwApc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApk">[</span>94<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApo">]</span></span></a></sup> Saudi Princess Alia bint Abdullah has been involved in the Saudi AIDS Society, which was permitted in December 2006 to hold a public charity art auction followed by a discussion on how the disease was impacting the kingdom; the event included participation by two Saudis living with HIV. It was organized with the help of the director-general of the AIDS Program at the Saudi Ministry of Health, Dr. Sana Filimban.<sup about="#mwt339" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-97" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-97"}}'><a href="#cite_note-97" id="mwAps"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAp0">[</span>96<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAp4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="17" id="mwAp8" aria-labelledby="2007"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="2007">2007</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: 2007"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAqA">In January 2007, a Saudi economics professor at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdulaziz_University" title="King Abdulaziz University" id="mwAqE">King Abdul Aziz University</a> was permitted to conduct of survey of a handful of Saudi University students on their level of education about the pandemic.<sup about="#mwt342" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-98" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-98"}}'><a href="#cite_note-98" id="mwAqI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqQ">[</span>97<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqU">]</span></span></a></sup> While much of the work on AIDS-HIV education has been supported by members of the Saudi royal family or medical doctors, there is an attempt to gain permission to create some independent AIDS societies, one of which is called Al-Husna Society, that would work on helping people infected with the disease find employment, education families and work to fight the prejudice that faces people infected.<sup about="#mwt345" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-99" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-99"}}'><a href="#cite_note-99" id="mwAqY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqg">[</span>98<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqk">]</span></span></a></sup> In 2007, a government-funded<sup about="#mwt348" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-USStateD_2002licence_100-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"USStateD_2002licence"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-USStateD_2002licence-100"}}'><a href="#cite_note-USStateD_2002licence-100" id="mwAqo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqw">[</span>99<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAq0">]</span></span></a></sup> organization, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Society_for_Human_Rights" title="National Society for Human Rights" id="mwAq4">National Society for Human Rights</a>, published a document suggesting ways to improve the treatment of people living with the disease. The proposed <i id="mwAq8">"Bill of Rights"</i> document was criticized by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch" id="mwArA">Human Rights Watch</a> for allegedly undermining human rights and global efforts to fight the pandemic.<sup about="#mwt351" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-101" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-101"}}'><a href="#cite_note-101" id="mwArE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArM">[</span>100<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="18" id="mwArU" aria-labelledby="2011"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="2011">2011</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: 2011"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwArY">According to a Gulf Cooperation Council report on HIV/AIDS, "Stigma and discrimination towards people living with HIV, weak surveillance system, travel restrictions and mandatory HIV testing along with gender inequalities were identified as critical issues that are hindering an effective AIDS response in the region."<sup about="#mwt354" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-102" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-102"}}'><a href="#cite_note-102" id="mwArc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArk">[</span>101<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAro">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="19" id="mwArs" aria-labelledby="Foreigners_and_HIV/AIDS"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Foreigners_and_HIV/AIDS"><span id="Foreigners_and_HIV.2FAIDS" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Foreigners and HIV/AIDS</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Foreigners and HIV/AIDS"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwArw">Foreigners who are applying for a work visa are required to demonstrate that they are not infected with the virus before they can enter the country, and are required to get a test upon arrival at a government accredited lab. To be issued their first work permit, they are also required to perform the test again before the renewal of a residency or work permit. Any foreigner that is discovered to be infected will be deported to the country of origin as soon as they are deemed fit to travel.<sup about="#mwt357" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-103" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-103"}}'><a href="#cite_note-103" id="mwAr0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAr4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAr8">[</span>102<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="20" id="mwAsE" aria-labelledby="Summary_table"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Summary_table">Summary table</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Summary table"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<table class="wikitable" id="mwAsI">

rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"pink apr 2019"}}'><a href="#cite_note-pink_apr_2019-6" id="mwAs8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtA"><span <li id="mwA1Q"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_in_Islam" title="LGBTQ in Islam" class="mw-redirect" id="mwA1U">LGBTQ in Islam</a></li>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="22" id="mwA1g" aria-labelledby="Notes"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Notes">Notes</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Notes"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt377" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA1k" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"notelist","href":"./Template:Notelist"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}'><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1327269900" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt378" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Reflist/styles.css"}}'>.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}body.skin-vector-2022 .mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:27em}body.skin-vector-2022 .mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:22.5em}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=upper-alpha]{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=upper-roman]{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=lower-alpha]{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=lower-greek]{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .references[data-mw-group=lower-roman]{list-style-type:lower-roman}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-upper-alpha .references{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-upper-roman .references{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-lower-alpha .references{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-lower-greek .references{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output div.reflist-liststyle-lower-roman .references{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style></span><div about="#mwt377" id="mwA1o">
<div class="mw-references-wrap" typeof="mw:Extension/references" about="#mwt379" id="mwA1s" data-mw='{"name":"references","attrs":{"group":"lower-alpha","responsive":"1"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'><ol class="mw-references references" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwA1w"><li about="#cite_note-7" id="cite_note-7" data-mw-footnote-number="a"><span class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwA10"><a href="#cite_ref-7" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" rel="mw:referencedBy" id="mwA14"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwA18">↑</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-7" class="mw-reference-text reference-text" data-mw-group="lower-alpha">The length of any prison sentence imposed is discretionary, not codified, nor dependent on precedent. Maximum terms of imprisonment are not ascertainable,<sup about="#mwt18" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-IDuA_5-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"IDuA"}}'><a href="#cite_note-IDuA-5" id="mwA2A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2E"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2I">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2M">]</span></span></a></sup> but <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_imprisonment" title="Life imprisonment" id="mwA2Q">life terms</a> may be possible.</span></li>
</ol></div></div>

Government of Saudi Arabia. Retrieved <span class="nowrap" id="mwBqw">16 August</span> 2021</span>. <q id="mwBq0">The laws of the Kingdom of Saudi Arabia, which are derived from Islamic Sharia, take the principle of complementary equality between men and women, which takes into account the characteristics of each sex and how each differs from the other, and ultimately achieves justice.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=Human+Rights+Commission&amp;rft.atitle=%D9%83%D9%8A%D9%81+%D8%AA%D8%AA%D9%85+%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%B3%D8%A7%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%A9+%D8%A8%D9%8A%D9%86+%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B1%D8%AC%D9%84+%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%B1%D8%A3%D8%A9+%D8%A8%D9%85%D9%88%D8%AC%D8%A8+%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%85%D8%A9+%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D9%85%D9%84%D9%83%D8%A9%D8%9F&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.hrc.gov.sa%2Far-sa%2FHumanRightsInSaudi%2FPages%2Fequalityrights.aspx&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ALGBTQ+rights+in+Saudi+Arabia" class="Z3988" id="mwBq4"></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:CS1_Arabic-language_sources_(ar)" id="mwBq8"/></li><li style="margin-bottom:.5em; display:block;;margin-top:.5em;" id="mwBrA"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt289" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" id="mwBrE" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Citation/CS1/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/><cite class="citation web cs1" id="mwBrI"><a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://www.hrc.gov.sa/website/hrc-in-ksa/1" class="external text" id="mwBrM">"Human rights in Saudi Arabia: Equal opportunities"</a>. <i id="mwBrQ">Human Rights Commission</i>. </div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="navbox-group" style="width:1%">Invalidated</th><td class="navbox-list-with-group navbox-list navbox-odd hlist" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em">
<ul><li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decriminalization_of_homosexuality_in_Ecuador" title="Decriminalization of homosexuality in Ecuador">Ecuador</a> (1871–<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Case_No._111-97-TC" title="Case No. 111-97-TC">1997</a>)</li>
<li>India (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Section_377_of_the_Indian_Penal_Code" title="Section 377 of the Indian Penal Code" class="mw-redirect">Section 377</a>, 1861–2018)</li>
<li>Sri Lanka (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_365_of_the_Sri_Lankan_Penal_Code" title="Article 365 of the Sri Lankan Penal Code">Article 365</a>, 1883–)</li>
<li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sodomy_laws_in_the_United_States" title="Sodomy laws in the United States">United States</a></li></ul>
</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="navbox-group" style="width:1%">Repealed</th><td class="navbox-list-with-group navbox-list navbox-even hlist" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em">
<ul><li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legality_of_same-sex_sexual_activity_in_Australia" title="Legality of same-sex sexual activity in Australia" class="mw-redirect">Australia</a> (1975/1997)</li>
<li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_status_of_homosexuality_in_Brazil" title="Legal status of homosexuality in Brazil">Brazil</a> (1533–1830)</li>
<li>Canada (?–<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_Law_Amendment_Act,_1968–69" title="Criminal Law Amendment Act, 1968–69">1969</a>)</li>
<li>Chile (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_365_of_the_Penal_Code_of_Chile" title="Article 365 of the Penal Code of Chile">Article 365</a>, 1875-1999/2022)</li>
<li>Cyprus (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Section_171_of_the_Criminal_Code_of_Cyprus" title="Section 171 of the Criminal Code of Cyprus">Section 171</a>, 1929–1998)</li>
<li>Germany (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paragraph_175" title="Paragraph 175">Paragraph 175</a>, 1871–1969/1994)</li>
<li>New Zealand (1840–<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homosexual_Law_Reform_Act_1986" title="Homosexual Law Reform Act 1986">1986</a>)</li>
<li>Singapore (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Section_377A_of_the_Penal_Code_(Singapore)" title="Section 377A of the Penal Code (Singapore)" class="mw-redirect">Section 377A</a>, 1938–2007/2022)</li>
<li>Norway (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Section_213_of_the_Norwegian_Penal_Code" title="Section 213 of the Norwegian Penal Code">Section 213</a>,<span typeof="mw:DisplaySpace"> </span>?–1972)</li>
<li>Romania (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_200" title="Article 200">Article 200</a>, 1968–1996/2001)</li>
<li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_history_in_Russia" title="LGBT history in Russia" class="mw-redirect">Russia</a> (1832–1917, 1933–1993)</li>
<li>South Africa (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Section_20A" title="Section 20A">Section 20A</a>, 1969–2007)</li>
<li>United Kingdom (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buggery_Act_1533" title="Buggery Act 1533">1533</a>–<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_Offences_Act_1967" title="Sexual Offences Act 1967">1967</a>/<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homosexual_Offences_(Northern_Ireland)_Order_1982" title="Homosexual Offences (Northern Ireland) Order 1982">1982</a>)</li></ul>
